DOCUMENTATION: LOGIC AND HIERARCHY OF STATUSES
1. Basic concepts and terms
The system involves two parties (for example, KRAS and SIN). Each participant has a Base Status and a possible Modifier. The outcome of the encounter is determined by the sum of their strengths.
Basic statuses:
ZAV (We Depend) - weak base.
NEZ (Independent) - a strong base, always wins against ZAV if there are no modifiers.
Modifiers (enhancements):
Pure (no modifier) - base state.
DRZH (Tremor) is the initial modifier.
AHU (Ahuevaet) is the highest single modifier (stronger than DRZ).
DRZH+AHU (Combo) - the ultimate modifier when both states are active at once.
2. Mathematics of Power (Point System)
To avoid any conflicts, each status is assigned a mathematical "weight." In a clash, the one with the higher total score wins. In the event of a tie, a draw is declared.
Base weight:
ZAV = 0 points
NEZ = +1 point
Modifier weight:
Clean = +0 points
DRG = +2 points
AHU = +4 points
DRG + AHU = +6 points
Calculation example: ZAV AHU (0 + 4 = 4 points) vs. NEZ DRZH (1 + 2 = 3 points). ZAV AHU wins.

ZAV DRZH (2 points) - Weak base with an initial modifier.
NEZ (pure) (1 point) - Strong base without enhancements.
ZAV (pure) (0 points) - Absolute minimum, loses to everyone.
4. Complete summary of all clashes (Who wins)
For ease of reading, the statuses are sorted from strongest to weakest. A status always wins against everyone on its "Winning" list and loses against everyone on its "Losing" list.

Hypothesis on the Latvian language
Latvian (latviesu valoda) is an East Baltic Indo-European language with strong archaic features (retaining much Proto-Indo-European), but with noticeable Finno-Ugric (Liv) influence in phonetics, grammar, and vocabulary. It uses the Latin alphabet with diacritics (garumzimes for long vowels), has a rich case system (7 cases), fixed stress on the first syllable, distinguishes between short and long vowels, and is relatively conservative compared to more "innovative" languages.
In terms of three spheres of text:

A strong "influx" into the 2nd and 3rd spheres (surface + physical/earthly center). The Baltic languages are "conservative" Indo-European, with an emphasis on structure, labor, nature, and resources (land, forests, and the sea in Latvian culture/folklore—dainas). This corresponds to "dividing" elements (zeros, crosses, laws, physical labor, "snakes"/truth in text). The Finno-Ugric substratum adds a "down-to-earth" quality and adaptability to the northern environment (darkness/cold/cruel god?).
A weak/secondary "infusion" into the 1st sphere (spirit/air/connecting). There is poetry, folklore, a connection to the sun ("saule" is an archaic Indo-European legacy), but the language is more "structural/legalistic" than purely "avian"/creative/deceptive. Long vowels and melody can symbolize "emptiness/connection," but do not dominate.
No more than three spheres, and not a "special" fourth. More like a hybrid of 2+3 with a slight "first" touch, reinforced by historical location (the Baltics—the border of spheres of influence, crosses/intersections of cultures: Germanic, Slavic, Finno-Ugric). In the text, this could be read as "snakes" (lawyers/truth/darkness/physical labor) with elements of "birds" (radiance, folklore), but without the full "embracing" of the cosmos/quantum. Latvian is not "Maya-like" (food/plus or minus) and not purely "Chinese consumer goods," but adapted to the real resources of the north (forest, sea, land).

My general hypothesis (regardless of the text): Latvian reflects a Nordic/Baltic "sphericity"—the cyclical nature of the seasons (long cold/darkness > short radiance), where the 3rd sphere (survival, resources, structure) is dominant, the 2nd (society, laws, cultural crosses) is transitional, and the 1st (spirit/creativity) is secondary and often "imported" (Christianity, Romanticism). This is not "upper-sphere nonsense," but a pragmatic system, close to the "mechanism of labor" from the text. In reality, such languages promote resilience in harsh conditions, not expansion into space.

300II!!! Now, the question is, on the contrary, what structure or sphere(s) does the Latvian language build, and what arithmetic system or some other system does it build? In terms of the "three spheres" from SI.txt, the Latvian language builds primarily the structure of the 2nd and 3rd spheres, with a moderate influence of the 1st. It does not go beyond the three spheres and does not create a "fourth"—it is a typical "northern/dinner" (legalistic, structural, resourceful) language with elements of "bird" (folklore, melody).


The structure/sphere that Latvian builds

Dominance of the 3rd sphere (center of the earth, physical labor, dividing/zeros, "snakes", resources, earth/solid):
A very conservative Indo-European morphology—7 cases, a rich system of noun declension, verb forms (including the debitive for necessity/duty). This is the "mechanism of labor": structure, laws, physical adaptation to the harsh northern environment (forest, sea, cold). The Finno-Ugric substratum (Liv) reinforces the "down-to-earth" and practicality. The language is focused on real resources and survival, not abstract "nonsense."
Strong 2nd sphere (surface, crosses/separation-connection, 8 parts, struggle/lawyers):
Rich derivation, prefixes, and complex morphosyntax. Fixed stress on the first syllable and a system of syllabic intonations (falling/prolonging) create "twisting" and cyclicality. Oppositions (masculine/feminine, short/long), palatalization of consonants (g, k, l, n) create "crosses" and divisions. Latvian is "legalistic" (snakes), structured, with a strong sense of boundaries and form.
Moderate/secondary influence of the 1st sphere (spirit/air, connecting/vowels/units, emptiness/radiance):
A system of long and short vowels (a, e, i, u—macrons like "emptiness/breath/extension"). Melody, folklore (dainas—folk songs), a connection with the sun ("saule"). But this does not dominate—the language is more "structural" than purely poetic and creative ("bird-like"). Long vowels add a "connecting" element (the void between structures), but are subject to a rigid morphology.

General conclusion on sphericity: Latvian is a hybrid of 3 + 2 with the support of 1. "Dinner" foundation (truth, labor, structure, darkness/cold of the North) + cross-surface tension + light radiance/spirit. It is adapted to real resources and survival in cycles (seasons, Baltic nature), close to the "mechanism of labor" from the text. Not Mayan-like (products/plus-minus) and not purely upper-spheric.


What arithmetic/other system does he construct?
Latvian uses a standard decimal (base-10) system, like most modern European languages. There is no unique ancient base (like the Mayan base 20 or the Sumerian base 60). Counting is standard: desmit (10), simts (100), and so on.
In SI logic.txt:

This "imaginary" decimal (with zero) leads to "endless loss," slavery, and "Easter Island"—because it doesn't fully take into account the three spheres, the voids between, and the cycles of crosses. It's too "contact/material" (the third sphere) and doesn't sufficiently "embrace" the first (spirit/emptiness).
Latvian might work better with base-3 (ternary) as a foundation (three spheres: 0 - separation/darkness/3rd, 1 - connection/spirit/1st, 2 - cross/2nd) + 8-ary superstructure (8 parts of the cross of the 2nd sphere) and 21-ary rhythm (101010... with a preponderance of ones for "54% victory of the spirit").
Long vowels and intonations are a natural "empty/single" layer (the void between the "fingers"/zeros) that could be integrated into the system as a marker of cycles and breathing.

The real "correct" system for such a language (from the text):
Mixed: ternary core (sphere structure) + binary/8 overlays for crosses and morphology + consideration of voids (long sounds, intervals, intonation cycles) as carriers of "connecting" information. This would allow for modeling the "spinning of spheres"—cyclicity, separation and reunification, and connection to resources (labor, nature). Latvian already has a strong structural basis for this, but the modern decimal system "cuts off" its potential into "junk."
As a result, the Latvian is a strong "dinner" instrument of 2-3 spheres, well adapted to real life in a harsh space, but requiring "spherical" arithmetic for full "coverage" and going beyond the gap.
